Strong convergence rates for full-discrete approximations of stochastic Burgers equations with multiplicative noise
Martin Hutzenthaler, Robert Link

Abstract
In this article we establish strong convergence rates on the whole probability space for explicit full-discrete approximations of stochastic Burgers equations with multiplicative trace-class noise. The key step in our proof is to establish uniform exponential moment estimates for the numerical approximations.

1
Introduction

Stochastic evolution equations (SEEs) are frequently used for modeling all
kinds of spatial dynamics with stochastic influence.
For example, stochastic Navier-Stokes equations are used as model
for
the motion of fluid parcels in turbulent and randomly forced fluid flows.
Nevertheless, to the best of our knowledge there exist no results in the scientific literature
establishing strong convergence rates on the whole probability space for an
explicit space-time discrete
approximation method
for SEEs with a non-globally monotone nonlinearity and multiplicative noise
such as
stochastic Burgers equations, stochastic Navier-Stokes equations,
Cahn-Hilliard-Cook equations, or
stochastic Kuramoto-Sivashinsky equations.
The key contribution of our following main result is to partially solve this problem
for stochastic Burgers equations.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
H=L^{2}((0,1);\mathbbm{R})
, let
A\colon D(A)\subseteq H\to H
be the Laplace operator
with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions on H,
let
T\in(0,\infty)
,
c\in\mathbbm{R}
,
\xi\in D(A)
,
let
(e_{k})_{k\in\mathbbm{N}}\subseteq H
satisfy for all
k\in\mathbbm{N}=\{1,2,\ldots\}

almost everywhere that
e_{k}(\cdot)=\sqrt{2}\sin(k\pi(\cdot))
,
let
(P_{N})_{N\in\mathbbm{N}}\subseteq L(H)
satisfy for all
N\in\mathbbm{N}
,
x\in H
that

P_{N}(x)=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\langle e_{k},x\rangle_{H}e_{k}
,
let
F\colon D((-A)^{\frac{1}{2}})\to H
satisfy for all
x\in D((-A)^{\frac{1}{2}})
that
F(x)=cx^{\prime}x
,
let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{P})
be a probability space with a normal filtration
\mathbb{F}=(\mathbb{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
,
let
(W_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
be an
\textup{Id}_{H}
-cylindrical
\mathbb{F}
-Wiener process,
let
Q\in L(H)
be a trace-class operator,
let
X\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to D((-A)^{\frac{1}{2}})

be an adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths such that for all
t\in[0,T]

it holds a.s. that

\begin{split}X_{t}=e^{tA}\xi+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)A}F(X_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)A}\sin(X_{s})Q\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},\end{split}

(1)

and
for all
N,n\in\mathbbm{N}
let
Y^{N,n}\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to H
be an adapted stochastic process
satisfying that for all
k\in\{0,1,\ldots,N-1\}
,
t\in(\frac{kT}{n},\frac{(k+1)T}{n}]
it holds a.s. that

Y_{0}^{N,n}=P_{N}(\xi)
and

\begin{split}Y_{t}^{N,n}={}&e^{(t-\frac{kT}{n})A}Y_{\frac{kT}{n}}^{N,n}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|(-A)^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\frac{kT}{n}}^{N,n}\|_{H}^{2}\leq(n/T)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\}}\int_{\frac{kT}{n}}^{t}e^{(t-s)A}P_{N}F(Y_{\frac{kT}{n}}^{N,n})\,\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\frac{\mathbbm{1}_{\{\|(-A)^{\frac{1}{2}}Y_{\frac{kT}{n}}^{N,n}\|_{H}^{2}\leq(n/T)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\}}\int_{\frac{kT}{n}}^{t}e^{(t-s)A}P_{N}\sin(X_{s})Q\,P_{N}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}}{1+\big\|\int_{\frac{kT}{n}}^{t}e^{(t-s)A}P_{N}\sin(X_{s})Q\,P_{N}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\big\|^{2}_{H}}.\end{split}

(2)

Then
for all
p\in(0,\infty)
there exists
C\in\mathbbm{R}
such that for all
N,n\in\mathbbm{N}
it holds that

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big(\mathbbm{E}\Big[\big\|X_{t}-Y_{t}^{N,n}\big\|^{p}_{H}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq C\big(N^{-1}+n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big).\end{split}

(3)

Theorem
1.1
follows from Corollary

5.7
1
1

1

with
B\curvearrowleft(H\ni x\mapsto\sin(x)Q\in HS(H,H))
.

The process
X
in Theorem
1.1
is a mild solution of the stochastic Burgers equation

\begin{split}dX_{t}(x)=\xi(x)+\Big(X_{t}^{\prime\prime}(x)+cX_{t}^{\prime}(x)X_{t}(x)\Big)\,dt+\sin(X_{t}(x))d(QW_{t})(x),\quad t\in[0,T],x\in(0,1)\end{split}

(4)

with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions driven by the trace-class noise
QW
.
We discretize space with Galerkin projections
P_{N}
,
N\in\mathbbm{N}
.
Time is discretized with uniform grids.
We note that the discretization method (
2
) does not discretize the semigroup

(e^{tA})_{t\in[0,T]}
. The reason for this is that
exponential Euler approximations have higher convergence rates;
see
[
35
]
, and
[
34
, Section 4]

for notes on the implementation.
The indicator function in (
2
) has no effect on most trajectories
(since
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|(-A)^{\frac{1}{2}}X_{t}\|_{H}<\infty
) and prevents the approximation from
exploding on exponentially unlikely events which could lead to divergence of moments; see
[
25
,
27
,
1
]
.
The denominator in the noise term in (
2
) is typically close to
1

and ensures that the normally distributed Brownian increments do not result in infinite exponential moments;
cf.
[
30
, Section 5]
.
The error estimate (
3
) shows that the approximations in (
2
)
converge in
L^{2}
with spatial rate
1-
and with temporal rate
1/2
to the solution process
X
.
Since the computational effort in computing
Y^{N,n}
is of order
N\cdot n
, we obtain an overall
rate

1/3
in terms of computational cost
(choose
N=K^{\frac{1}{3}}
and
n=K^{\frac{2}{3}}
to get total cost
K
and an error of order
K^{-\frac{1}{3}}
).
This strong error estimate together with the multilevel Monte Carlo
method in
[
19
]
also yields convergence rates for
multilevel Monte Carlo approximations of expectations of functions of
the solution of (
1
). This together with the representation result in

[
32
, Theorem 1.1]
yields
convergence rates of approximations of viscosity solutions of the
Kolmogorov backward equation associated with (
1
).

The literature on strong convergence rates for stochastic differential equations
with superlinearly
growing coefficients grows steadily in the last decade.
Moments of
explicit (exponential) Euler approximations diverge in this case;
see
[
25
,
27
,
1
]
.
To overcome this issue, tamed Euler approximations were introduced
in
[
26
,
23
]
.
Subsequently further tamed Euler approximations were introduced and analyzed;
see, e.g.,
[
10
,
31
,
40
,
41
,
43
,
44
,
47
]

for stochastic ordinary differential equtaions
and, e.g.,
[
2
,
3
,
21
,
29
,
38
,
39
,
42
]
for SEEs.
Strong convergence rates for explicit time discrete and explicit space-time discrete numerical methods for SEEs with a non-globally Lipschitz continuous but globally monotone nonlinearity have been derived in, e.g.,

[
2
,
3
,
7
,
38
,
45
]
.
Strong convergence rates
for
approximations of SEEs with non-globally monotone coefficients
on suitable large subsets of the probability space
(sometimes referred to as semi-strong convergence rates)
have been established in, e.g.,

[
4
,
8
,
18
]
.
Semi-strong convergence yields convergence in probability.
However, semi-strong convergence does not imply strong convergence.
Moreover,
strong
convergence with rates have been established
for
fully drift-implicit Euler
approximations
in the case of 2D stochastic
Navier-Stokes equations with additive trace-class noise
by exploiting a rather specific property (see Bessaih & Millet
[
5
, (2.4) in Section 2]
)
of the Navier-Stokes-nonlinearity;
see
[
5
,
6
]
.
These fully drift-implicit Euler approximations
involve solutions of nonlinear equations that are not known to be unique
and it is unclear how to efficiently solve these nonlienar equations numerically.
Strong convergence rates for nonlinear-implicit numerical schemes for SEEs with non-globally monotone coefficients have also been analyzed in

[
16
,
12
,
16
,
15
,
13
]

(cf. also, e.g.,
[
14
,
46
]
).
The case of additive trace-class noise is by now better understood.
Theorem 5.9 in
[
28
]
proves strong convergence rates for
SEEs with additive noise if – roughly speaking –
the nonlinearity
F
has has at most one-sided linear growth as function from
H
to
H
,
the drift
A+F
is globally one-sided Lipschitz continuous as function
from
H
to
H_{\frac{1}{2}}
,
and the nonlinearity
F

is locally Lipschitz continuous with at most polynomially growing local
Lipschitz constant as function from
H
to
H_{\frac{1}{2}}
.
In particular,
[
28
, Theorem 1.1]
establishes strong convergence rates
for stochastic Burgers equations with additive trace-class noise.
The method of proof of
[
28
]

is to subtract the noise term and to analyze the resulting
Hilbert-space-valued random partial differential equation.
This approach cannot be extended or adapted to the case of non-additive noise.
For this reason, it remained an open problem how to establish strong convergence rates
in the multiplicative noise case.

The remainder of this article is organized as follows. In
Section

2

we establish exponential moment estimates for SPDEs
which are a central ingredient in our analysis.

Section

3
provides (exponential) moment estimates for tamed exponential Euler approximations.

Section

4
provides a perturbation estimate for stochastic differential equations.
Finally, in
Section

5
we apply these results to
the case of stochastic Burgers equations with zero Dirichlet boundary
conditions and bounded multiplicative noise.
Throughout this article,
we denote by
\infty^{0}
,
0^{0}
,
0\cdot\infty
the real numbers satisfying that

\infty^{0}=1
,
0^{0}=1
, and that
0\cdot\infty=0
.

2
Exponential moment estimates for stochastic integrals and
perturbed SPDEs

In this section we establish exponential moments bound of
solutions of perturbed
SPDEs.

Lemma

2.2
and
Lemma

2.3
provide
elementary semigroup estimates.
In

Lemma

2.4
and
Lemma

2.5

we derive
exponential moment bounds for stochastic integrals, which we later need
to show the assumptions
of Lemma
2.7
.
Lemma
2.6
then
generalizes Corollary 2.2 in Jentzen & Pušnik
[
37
]

and derives
an exponential moment estimate for stochastic processes having
a one step exponential moment bound.
Moreover,
Lemma
2.7

proves an exponential moment bound for perturbed SPDEs
and
will
be used to
deduce a one step exponential moment bound for tamed exponential Euler approximations.

Setting 2.1
.

Let
T\in(0,\infty)
,
let
\theta\subseteq[0,T]
satisfy that

\{0,T\}\subseteq\theta
and that
\#\theta<\infty
,
let
|\theta|\in[0,T]
be the real number satisfying that

|\theta|=\max\{x\in(0,\infty)\colon(\exists a,b\in\theta\colon[x=b-a\textrm{ and }\theta\cap(a,b)=\emptyset])\},

(5)

let
\llcorner\cdot\lrcorner_{\theta}\colon[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)

satisfy for all
t\in(0,\infty)
that

\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}=\max\{[0,t)\cap\theta\}
,
and that
\llcorner 0\lrcorner_{\theta}=0
,
let

(H,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{H},\|\cdot\|_{H})
and

(U,\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{U},\|\cdot\|_{U})

be separable
\mathbbm{R}
-Hilbert spaces with
\#H\wedge\#U>1
,
let
\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbbm{N}
,
let
(\lambda_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\subseteq(-\infty,0)
,
satisfy that

0>\sup_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\lambda_{i}\geq\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\lambda_{i}>-\infty,

let
(e_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\subseteq H
be an orthonormal basis of
H
,
let
A\colon H\to H
be
a linear operator
satisfying for all

x\in H
that

Ax=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\lambda_{i}\langle x,e_{i}\rangle_{H}e_{i}
,
let
\|\cdot\|_{H_{r}}\colon H\to\mathbbm{R}
,
r\in\mathbbm{R}
, be a norm satisfying
for all
x\in H
and all
r\in\mathbbm{R}
that

\|x\|_{H_{r}}=\|(-A)^{r}x\|_{H}
,
let

(\tilde{e}_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{J}}\subseteq U
, be an orthonormal basis of
U
,
let

(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbbm{P})

be a probability space with a normal filtration

\mathbb{F}=(\mathbb{F}_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}
,
and let

(W_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}

be an
\operatorname{Id}_{U}
-cylindrical

\mathbb{F}
-Wiener
process with continuous sample paths.

Lemma 2.2
.

Assume Setting
2.1
and let

x\in H
,

\gamma\in[0,1]
,
\delta\in\mathbbm{R}
,
t\in[0,T]
.
Then it holds that

\big\|(e^{tA}-\operatorname{Id}_{H})x\big\|_{H_{\delta}}\leq\|x\|_{H_{\delta+\gamma}}t^{\gamma}.

(6)

Proof.

The fact that

\forall\lambda\in(0,\infty)\colon\lambda^{-\gamma}(1-e^{-\lambda})\leq 1

implies that

\begin{split}&\big\|(e^{tA}-\operatorname{Id}_{H})x\big\|_{H_{\delta}}\leq\|x\|_{H_{\delta+\gamma}}\big\|A^{-\gamma}(e^{tA}-\operatorname{Id}_{H})\big\|_{L(H_{\delta},H_{\delta})}\\
\leq{}&\|x\|_{H_{\delta+\gamma}}\sup_{\lambda\in(0,\infty)}\big(\lambda^{-\gamma}(1-e^{-t\lambda})\big)\leq\|x\|_{H_{\delta+\gamma}}t^{\gamma}.\end{split}

(7)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
2.2
.
∎

Lemma 2.3
.

Assume Setting
2.1
and let
x\in H
,

\gamma\in[0,1)
,
\delta\in\mathbbm{R}
,

t\in[0,T]
.
Then it holds that

\Big\|\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}x\,\mathrm{d}s\Big\|_{H_{\delta}}\leq(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{\gamma}\tfrac{(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})^{1-\gamma}}{1-\gamma}\|x\|_{H_{\delta-\gamma}}.

(8)

Proof.

The fact that

\forall\lambda\in(0,\infty)\colon\lambda^{\gamma}e^{-\lambda}\leq(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{\gamma}

shows
that

\begin{split}&\Big\|\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}x\,\mathrm{d}s\Big\|_{H_{\delta}}\\
\leq{}&\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\|(-A)^{\gamma}e^{(t-s)A}\|_{L(H_{\delta},H_{\delta})}\,\|x\|_{H_{\delta-\gamma}}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\big(\sup_{\lambda\in(0,\infty)}\lambda^{\gamma}e^{-(t-s)\lambda}\big)\|x\|_{H_{\delta-\gamma}}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{\gamma}(t-s)^{-\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\|x\|_{H_{\delta-\gamma}}\\
={}&(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{\gamma}\tfrac{(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})^{1-\gamma}}{1-\gamma}\|x\|_{H_{\delta-\gamma}}.\end{split}

(9)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
2.3
.
∎

Lemma 2.4
.

Assume Setting
2.1
,
and let
Q\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to HS(U,H)
be progressively measurable.
Then it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(\int_{0}^{T}\big\|\int_{0}^{s}(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\big\|_{H}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\big)\Big]\\
\leq{}&\tfrac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{\big(1-4\,eT\big(\big(\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\|Q(u)-Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}}{e\cdot(s-u)}\,\mathrm{d}u\big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\|Q(s)\|_{HS(U,H)}\big)^{2}\big)^{+}}\Bigg]\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(10)

Proof.

First note that the Jensen inequality
and the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see, e.g., Theorem A in
[
9
]
) imply that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(\int_{0}^{T}\big\|\int_{0}^{s}(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\big\|^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s\big)\Big]\\
=&\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{1}{n!}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|\int_{0}^{s}(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\|^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{n}\Big]\\
={}&1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{T^{n}}{n!}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\Big(\tfrac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\|\int_{0}^{s}(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\|^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{n}\Big]\\
\leq{}&1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{T^{n}}{n!}\tfrac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\int_{0}^{s}(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\|^{2n}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big]\\
={}&1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{T^{n-1}}{n!}\int_{0}^{T}\Big\|\int_{0}^{s}(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\Big\|^{2n}_{L^{2n}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{T^{n-1}}{n!}\int_{0}^{T}\Big(2\sqrt{2n}\,\Big\|\big(\int_{0}^{s}\|(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}u\big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{2n}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\Big)^{2n}\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(11)

Moreover, the triangle inequality and
the fact that
\sup_{x\in(0,\infty)}e^{-x}x\leq e^{-1}

show
for all
s\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\|(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
={}&\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\|(Q(u))^{*}e^{A(s-u)}(-A)^{1/2}e_{i}\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
\leq{}&\Big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{0}^{s}e^{2\lambda_{i}(s-u)}(-\lambda_{i})\,\|(Q(u)-Q(s))^{*}e_{i}\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
&\quad+\Big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{0}^{s}e^{2\lambda_{i}(s-u)}(-\lambda_{i})\,\|(Q(s))^{*}e_{i}\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
={}&\Big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{0}^{s}e^{2\lambda_{i}(s-u)}\tfrac{2(-\lambda_{i})(s-u)}{2(s-u)}\,\|(Q(u)-Q(s))^{*}e_{i}\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
&\quad+\Big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(1-\tfrac{e^{2\lambda_{i}s}}{2})\,\|(Q(s))^{*}e_{i}\|^{2}_{U}\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
\leq{}&\Big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{0}^{s}\tfrac{\|(Q(u)-Q(s))^{*}e_{i}\|^{2}_{U}}{2e\cdot(s-u)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\Big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(1-\tfrac{e^{2\lambda_{i}s}}{2})\,\|(Q(s))^{*}e_{i}\|^{2}_{U}\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
\leq{}&\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\tfrac{\|Q(u)-Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}}{2e\cdot(s-u)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\tfrac{\|Q(s)\|_{HS(U,H)}}{\sqrt{2}}.\end{split}

(12)

Combining (
11
) and (
12
)
proves that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(\int_{0}^{T}\big\|\int_{0}^{s}(-A)^{1/2}e^{A(s-u)}Q(u)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\big\|^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}s\big)\Big]\\
\leq{}&1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{T^{n-1}}{n!}\int_{0}^{T}\Big(2\sqrt{2n}\,\Big\|\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\tfrac{\|Q(u)-Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}}{2e\cdot(s-u)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\tfrac{\|Q(s)\|_{HS(U,H)}}{\sqrt{2}}\Big\|_{L^{2n}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\Big)^{2n}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
={}&1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{8^{n}n^{n}T^{n-1}}{n!}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\Big(\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\tfrac{\|Q(u)-Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}}{2e\cdot(s-u)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\tfrac{\|Q(s)\|_{HS(U,H)}}{\sqrt{2}}\Big)^{2n}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&\tfrac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\frac{8^{n}n^{n}T^{n}}{\sqrt{2\pi n}\big(\tfrac{n}{e}\big)^{n}}\Big(\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\tfrac{\|Q(u)-Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}}{2e\cdot(s-u)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\tfrac{\|Q(s)\|_{HS(U,H)}}{\sqrt{2}}\Big)^{2n}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&\tfrac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}4^{n}e^{n}T^{n}\Big(\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\tfrac{\|Q(u)-Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}}{e\cdot(s-u)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\|Q(s)\|_{HS(U,H)}\Big)^{2n}\Big]\,\mathrm{d}s\\
={}&\tfrac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{\Big(1-4\,eT\big(\big(\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\|Q(u)-Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}}{e\cdot(s-u)}\,\mathrm{d}u\big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+\|Q(s)\|_{HS(U,H)}\big)^{2}\Big)^{+}}\Bigg]\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(13)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
2.4
.
∎

Lemma 2.5
.

Assume Setting
2.1
,
let

Q\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to HS(U,H)
be progressively measurable,
and let
t\in[0,T]
.
Then it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(\big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\big\|_{H}\big)\Big]\leq 2\,\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(2e\int_{0}^{t}\|e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big].

Proof.

First note that for all
x\in\mathbbm{R}
it holds that

\exp(x)\leq\exp(x)+\exp(-x)=2\Big(1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{x^{2n}}{(2n)!}\Big).

(14)

Thus, the Jensen inequality and the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see, e.g., Theorem A in
[
9
]
)
verify that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(\big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\big\|_{H}\big)\Big]\\
\leq&2\,\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\Big(\big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\big\|_{H}\Big)^{2n}\Big]\\
={}&2+2\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\|^{2n}_{H}\Big]\\
={}&2+2\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big\|^{2n}_{L^{2n}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\\
\leq{}&2+2\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\Big(2\sqrt{2n}\,\Big\|\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{2n}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\Big)^{2n}.\end{split}

(15)

Therefore, the fact that

\forall n\in\mathbbm{N}\colon\sqrt{2\pi n}\big(\tfrac{n}{e}\big)^{n}\leq n!\leq\tfrac{12}{11}\sqrt{2\pi n}\big(\tfrac{n}{e}\big)^{n}\leq\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2\pi n}\big(\tfrac{n}{e}\big)^{n}

proves that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(\big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\big\|_{H}\big)\Big]\\
\leq{}&2+2\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{1}{(2n)!}\Big(2\sqrt{2n}\,\Big\|\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{2n}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\Big)^{2n}\\
\leq{}&2+2\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\tfrac{8^{n}n^{n}}{(2n)!}\Big(\Big\|\big(\int_{0}^{t}\|e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{2n}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\Big)^{2n}\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[2+2\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\frac{8^{n}n^{n}}{\sqrt{4\pi n}\big(\tfrac{2n}{e}\big)^{2n}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{n}\Big]\\
={}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[2+2\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\frac{e^{n}\cdot 2^{n}}{\sqrt{2}\sqrt{2\pi n}\big(\tfrac{n}{e}\big)^{n}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{n}\Big]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[2+2\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}\frac{e^{n}\cdot 2^{n}}{n!}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\|e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{n}\Big]\\
={}&2\,\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(2e\int_{0}^{t}\|e^{A(t-s)}Q(s)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big].\end{split}

(16)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
2.5
.
∎

The next lemma generalizes Corollary 2.2 in Jentzen & Pušnik
[
37
]

to more general stochastic processes.

Lemma 2.6
.

Assume Setting
2.1
,
let
\rho,c\in[0,\infty)
,
let

E\in\mathcal{B}(H)
be a set,
let
S\colon(0,T]\to L(H)
,
let
V\in\operatorname{C}^{1,2}([0,T]\times H,[0,\infty))
,
let
\overline{V}\colon[0,T]\times H\to\mathbbm{R}
be measurable,
let
Y\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to H

be measurable and adapted,
assume that for all
t\in(0,T]
,
r\in[t,T]
,

x\in H
it holds that

V(r,S_{t}x)\leq V(r-t,x),

and

\mathbbm{1}_{H\backslash E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\big(Y_{t}-S_{t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\big)=0,

(17)

and assume
that for all
t\in(0,T]
it holds a.s. that

\int_{0}^{T}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\lfloor s\rfloor_{\theta}})|\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})|\,\mathrm{d}s<\infty

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{V(t,Y_{t})}{e^{\rho(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}))}}+\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\int_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{t}\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho(s-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}))}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)~\Big|(Y_{r})_{r\in[0,{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}]}\Big]\\
\leq{}&e^{\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})}\cdot e^{c(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})}.\end{split}

(18)

Then it holds for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\begin{split}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{V(t,Y_{t})}{e^{\rho t}}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big]\leq{}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(V(0,Y_{0})+ct\big)\Big].\end{split}

(19)

Proof of Lemma
2.6
.

It follows from (
17
)
that for all
t\in(0,T]
it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{V(t,Y_{t})}{e^{\rho t}}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big]\\
={}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{H\backslash E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{V(t,Y_{t})}{e^{\rho t}}+\int_{0}^{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s+\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{V(t,Y_{t})}{e^{\rho t}}\\
\quad&+\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\int_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{t}\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big]\\
={}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{\mathbbm{1}_{H\backslash E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V\big(t,S_{t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\big)}{e^{\rho t}}+\int_{0}^{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\\
\quad&\cdot\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(t,Y_{t})}{e^{\rho(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})}}+\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\int_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{t}\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho(s-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{e^{-\rho\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}}~\Big|(Y_{r})_{r\in[0,{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}]}\Big]\Big].\end{split}

(20)

Thus, the Jensen inequality and
(
18
) verify
for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{V(t,Y_{t})}{e^{\rho t}}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{\mathbbm{1}_{H\backslash E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})}{e^{\rho\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}}+\int_{0}^{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\\
\quad&\cdot\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(t,Y_{t})}{e^{\rho(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}))}}+\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\int_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{t}\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho(s-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)~\Big|(Y_{r})_{r\in[0,{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}]}\Big]^{e^{-\rho\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}}\Big]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{\mathbbm{1}_{H\backslash E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})}{e^{\rho\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}}+\int_{0}^{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\\
\quad&\cdot\exp\Big(\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})+c(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})\Big)^{e^{-\rho\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}}\Big]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(\tfrac{V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})}{e^{\rho\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}}+\int_{0}^{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tfrac{\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})}{e^{\rho s}}\,\mathrm{d}s+c(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})\Big)\Big]\\
\leq{}&\ldots\leq\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(V(0,Y_{0})+ct\big)\Big].\end{split}

(21)

This completes the proof of Lemma
2.6
.
∎

In the next lemma we derive
exponential moments estimates of
solutions of perturbed
SPDEs.

Lemma 2.7
.

Assume Setting
2.1
,
let
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},c_{5},c_{6},c_{7}\in[0,\infty)
,

\alpha\in(-\infty,0]
,

\gamma\in\mathbbm{R}^{7}
,

h\in(0,1\wedge T]
,
let
E\in\mathcal{B}(H)
,
let
V\in\operatorname{C}^{1,2}([0,T]\times H,[0,\infty))
,
let

\overline{V}\colon[0,T]\times H\to\mathbbm{R}
,

F\colon[0,T]\times H\to H
, and

B\colon[0,T]\times H\to HS(U,H)
be measurable,
let

X,Y\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to H

be maesurable and adapted,
let

Z\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to HS(U,H)

be progressively measurable,
assume that for all
t\in[0,T]
it hold a.s. that

\int_{0}^{T}\|AX_{s}+Y_{s}\|_{H}+\|Z_{s}\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}+|\overline{V}(s,X_{s})|\,\mathrm{d}s<\infty
and

X_{t}=X_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}AX_{s}+Y_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}Z_{s}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},

(22)

assume that for all
t\in[0,T]

it holds that

\begin{split}&(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial t}V)(t,X_{t})+\langle\nabla_{x}V(t,X_{t}),F(t,X_{t})+A(X_{t})\rangle_{H}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|B^{*}(t,X_{t})\,\nabla_{x}V(t,X_{t})\|^{2}_{U}\\
&\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle B(t,X_{t}),(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(t,X_{t})B(t,X_{t})\rangle_{HS(U,H)}+\overline{V}(t,X_{t})\leq 0,\end{split}

(23)

assume that for all
s\in(0,h]
and all
x,y\in H

it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(Y_{s}-F(s,X_{s}))\|_{L^{4}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\alpha})}\leq c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}},

(24)

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(Z_{s}-B(s,X_{s}))\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}\leq c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}},

(25)

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})((\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s}))\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};L(H_{\alpha},\mathbbm{R}))}\leq c_{2}h^{\gamma_{2}},

(26)

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})((\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s}))\|_{L^{4}(\mathbbm{P};L(H,H))}\leq c_{2}h^{\gamma_{2}},

(27)

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot\exp\big(\int_{0}^{s}\overline{V}(r,X_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r\big)\Big\|_{L^{4}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\leq e^{c_{3}s^{\gamma_{3}}},

(28)

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(Z_{s})\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}\leq c_{4}h^{\gamma_{4}},

(29)

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(X_{s}-X_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\leq c_{4}h^{\gamma_{7}},

(30)

\displaystyle\big|V(0,x)-V(s,y)\big|\leq c_{5}(\|x-y\|_{H}+s)(1+V(0,x)+\|x-y\|_{H}^{c_{7}}),

(31)

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})V(0,X_{0})\leq c_{1}h^{\gamma_{6}},

(32)

and assume that for all
s\in(0,h]
and all
k\in(0,\infty)

it holds a.s. that

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\Big(\exp\big(k\cdot(\|X_{s}-X_{0}\|_{H}+s)\big)\Big)~\Big|~X_{0}\Big]\leq e^{c_{6}(k^{2}h^{2\gamma_{5}}+kh^{\gamma_{5}})}.

(33)

Then it holds for all
t\in(0,h]
that

\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})V(t,X_{t})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\overline{V}(s,X_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big)\Big]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\big[e^{\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})V(0,X_{0})}\big]\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathbbm{E}\Bigl[e^{4\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,V(0,X_{0})+c_{6}(16c^{2}_{5}(1+c_{1}h^{\gamma_{6}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}h^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{7}})^{2}h^{2\gamma_{5}}+4c_{5}(1+c_{1}h^{\gamma_{6}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}h^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{7}})h^{\gamma_{5}})}\Bigr]\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\\
&\qquad\qquad e^{c_{3}s^{\gamma_{3}}}\cdot c_{1}c_{2}h^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\Big(1+\tfrac{1}{2}\big(c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}}+2c_{4}h^{\gamma_{4}}\big)\big((\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}c_{2}h^{\gamma_{2}}+1\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(34)

Proof.

W.l.o.g. we assume that
X
has continuous sample paths.
First, denote by
\xi\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to\mathbbm{R}

the function satisfying for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\xi_{t}=\exp\Bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})V(t,X_{t})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\overline{V}(s,X_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\Bigr)

(35)

and by
\tau_{n}\colon\Omega\to[0,T]
,
n\in\mathbbm{N}
,
the stopping times satisfying for all

n\in\mathbbm{N}
that

\tau_{n}=\inf\Bigl(\Big\{t\in[0,T]\colon\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}\xi_{s}\cdot(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})\,Z_{s}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big\|_{H}>n\Big\}\cup\{T\}\Bigr).

(36)

Then Ito’s formula implies
for all
t\in[0,T]

and all
n\in\mathbbm{N}
that

\begin{split}&\xi_{(t\wedge\tau_{n})}\\
={}&\xi_{0}+\int_{0}^{(t\wedge\tau_{n})}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\xi_{s}\cdot(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})\,Z_{s}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\\
&+\int_{0}^{(t\wedge\tau_{n})}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot\xi_{s}\big((\tfrac{\partial}{\partial s}V)(s,X_{s})+\overline{V}(s,X_{s})+(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})(A(X_{s})+F(s,X_{s}))\\
&\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle B(s,X_{s}),(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})B(s,X_{s})\rangle_{HS(U,H)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{0}^{(t\wedge\tau_{n})}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot\xi_{s}\big((\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})(Y_{s}-F(s,X_{s}))-\tfrac{1}{2}\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\\
&\qquad-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle B(s,X_{s}),(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})B(s,X_{s})\rangle_{HS(U,H)}\\
&\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle Z_{s},(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})Z_{s}\rangle_{HS(U,H)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|Z_{s}^{*}\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\big)\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(37)

This and (
23
)
show for all
t\in[0,T]

and all
n\in\mathbbm{N}
that

\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}[\xi_{(t\wedge\tau_{n})}]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}[\xi_{0}]+\mathbbm{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{(t\wedge\tau_{n})}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\xi_{s}\Big((\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})(Y_{s}-F(s,X_{s}))+\tfrac{1}{2}\|Z_{s}^{*}\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\\
&\qquad-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle B(s,X_{s}),(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})B(s,X_{s})\rangle_{HS(U,H)}\\
&\qquad+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle Z_{s},(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})Z_{s}\rangle_{HS(U,H)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg].\end{split}

(38)

Thus, Fatou’s lemma
and Hölder’s inequality
establish for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\begin{split}\mathbbm{E}[\xi_{t}]\leq{}&\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbbm{E}[\xi_{(t\wedge\tau_{n})}]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}[\xi_{0}]+\mathbbm{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{t}\bigg|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\xi_{s}\Big((\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})(Y_{s}-F(s,X_{s}))\\
&-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle B(s,X_{s}),(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})B(s,X_{s})\rangle_{HS(U,H)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|Z_{s}^{*}\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\\
&+\tfrac{1}{2}\langle Z_{s},(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})Z_{s}\rangle_{HS(U,H)}-\tfrac{1}{2}\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\Big)\bigg|\,\mathrm{d}s\bigg]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}[\xi_{0}]+\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\xi_{s}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\Big(\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})(Y_{s}-F(s,X_{s}))\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
&+\tfrac{1}{2}\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot\langle B(s,X_{s}),(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})B(s,X_{s})\rangle_{HS(U,H)}\\
&\qquad-\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot\langle Z_{s},(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})Z_{s}\rangle_{HS(U,H)}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
&+\tfrac{1}{2}\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}-\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot\|Z^{*}_{s}\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(39)

Next we will estimate each term separately.
Therefore, note that it follows from
Hölder’s inequality,
(
28
),
(
31
), (
30
),
(
32
) and from
(
33
)
that for all

s\in(0,h]

it holds that

\begin{split}&\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\xi_{s}\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}=\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\exp\Bigl(V(s,X_{s})+\int_{0}^{s}\overline{V}(r,X_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r\Bigr)\Big\|^{4}_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\exp\bigl(V(s,X_{s})\bigr)\Big\|^{4}_{L^{4}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{s}\overline{V}(r,X_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)\Big\|^{4}_{L^{4}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Bigl[e^{4V(0,X_{0})}\,\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\exp\Bigl(4|V(s,X_{s})-V(0,X_{0})|\Bigr)\Bigr]e^{4c_{3}s^{\gamma_{3}}}\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Bigl[e^{4\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,V(0,X_{0})}\mathbbm{E}\Bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\\
&\qquad\cdot\exp\Bigl(4c_{5}(1+V(0,X_{0})+\|X_{s}-X_{0}\|_{H}^{c_{7}})(\|X_{s}-X_{0}\|_{H}+s)\Bigr)\Bigr]\Big|~X_{0}\Bigr]e^{4c_{3}s^{\gamma_{3}}}\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Bigl[e^{4\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,V(0,X_{0})}\mathbbm{E}\Bigl[\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\\
&\qquad\cdot\exp\Bigl(4c_{5}(1+c_{1}h^{\gamma_{6}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}h^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{7}})(\|X_{s}-X_{0}\|_{H}+s)\Bigr)\Bigr]\Big|~X_{0}\Bigr]e^{4c_{3}s^{\gamma_{3}}}\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Bigl[e^{4\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,V(0,X_{0})+c_{6}(16c^{2}_{5}(1+c_{1}h^{\gamma_{6}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}h^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{7}})^{2}h^{2\gamma_{5}}+4c_{5}(1+c_{1}h^{\gamma_{6}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}h^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{7}})h^{\gamma_{5}})}\Bigr]e^{4c_{3}s^{\gamma_{3}}}.\end{split}

(40)

Moreover, Hölder’s inequality, (
26
), and
(
24
)
imply for all

s\in(0,h]
that

\begin{split}&\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\cdot(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})(Y_{s}-F(s,X_{s}))\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\big\|\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})(-A)^{-\alpha}\|_{L(H,\mathbbm{R})}\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(-A)^{\alpha}(Y_{s}-F(s,X_{s}))\|_{H}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})\|_{L^{4}(\mathbbm{P};L(H_{\alpha},\mathbbm{R}))}\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(Y_{s}-F(s,X_{s}))\|_{L^{4}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\alpha})}\\
\leq{}&c_{1}c_{2}h^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}.\end{split}

(41)

Furthermore,
Hölder’s inequality,
(
25
),
(
27
), and
(
29
)
show for all

s\in(0,h]
that

\begin{split}&\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\langle B(s,X_{s}),(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})B(s,X_{s})\rangle_{HS(U,H)}\\
&\quad-\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\langle Z_{s},(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})Z_{s}\rangle_{HS(U,H)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
={}&\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\langle B(s,X_{s})-Z_{s},(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})(B(s,X_{s})+Z_{s})\rangle_{HS(U,H)}\\
\leq{}&\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(B(s,X_{s})-Z_{s})\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,(\operatorname{Hess}_{x}V)(s,X_{s})\|_{L^{4}(\mathbbm{P};L(H,H))}\\
&\quad\cdot\big(\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(B(s,X_{s})-Z_{s})\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}+2\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,Z_{s}\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}\big)\\
\leq{}&c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}}c_{2}h^{\gamma_{2}}(c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}}+2c_{4}h^{\gamma_{4}})=c_{1}c_{2}h^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}(c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}}+2c_{4}h^{\gamma_{4}}).\end{split}

(42)

In addition,
Hölder’s inequality,
(
26
),
(
25
), and (
29
)
verify for all
s\in(0,h]
that

\begin{split}&\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}-\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\|Z^{*}_{s}\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{U}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\big\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})-Z^{*}_{s}\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\big\|_{U}\\
&\quad\cdot\big\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})+Z^{*}_{s}\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\big\|_{U}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
={}&\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\big\|(B^{*}(s,X_{s})-Z^{*}_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\big\|_{U}\|(B^{*}(s,X_{s})+Z^{*}_{s})\,\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|_{U}\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\|\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|_{H_{-\alpha}}\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})-Z^{*}_{s}\|_{L(H_{-\alpha},U)}\\
&\cdot\|\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|_{H_{-\alpha}}\big(\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})+Z^{*}_{s}\|_{L(H_{-\alpha},U)}\big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\|\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|_{H_{-\alpha}}\|(-A)^{\alpha}\|_{L(H,H)}\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})-Z^{*}_{s}\|_{L(H,U)}\\
&\cdot\|\nabla_{x}V(s,X_{s})\|_{H_{-\alpha}}\big(\|(-A)^{\alpha}\|_{L(H,H)}\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})+Z^{*}_{s}\|_{L(H,U)}\big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,\|(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{L(H_{\alpha},\mathbbm{R})}(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})-Z^{*}_{s}\|_{HS(H,U)}\\
&\quad\big(\|B^{*}(s,X_{s})+Z^{*}_{s}\|_{HS(H,U)}\big)\Big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,(\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}V)(s,X_{s})\|^{2}_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};L(H_{\alpha},\mathbbm{R}))}\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})(B(s,X_{s})-Z_{s})\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}\\
&\quad\cdot\big(\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,B(s,X_{s})\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}+\|\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,Z_{s}\|_{L^{8}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}\big)\\
\leq{}&(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}c_{2}^{2}h^{2\gamma_{2}}c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}}\big(c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}}+c_{4}h^{\gamma_{4}}+c_{4}h^{\gamma_{4}}\big)=(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}c_{1}c_{2}^{2}h^{2\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{1}}\big(c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}}+2c_{4}h^{\gamma_{4}}\big).\end{split}

(43)

Combining (
39
),
(
40
),
(
41
)
(
42
),
and (
43
)
proves for all

t\in(0,h]
that

\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}[\xi_{t}]\leq{}\mathbbm{E}\big[e^{\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})V(0,X_{0})}\big]\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathbbm{E}\Bigl[e^{4\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{E}(X_{0})\,V(0,X_{0})+c_{6}(16c^{2}_{5}(1+c_{1}h^{\gamma_{6}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}h^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{7}})^{2}h^{2\gamma_{5}}+4c_{5}(1+c_{1}h^{\gamma_{6}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}h^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{7}})h^{\gamma_{5}})}\Bigr]\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot e^{c_{3}s^{\gamma_{3}}}c_{1}c_{2}h^{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\Big(1+\tfrac{1}{2}\big(c_{1}h^{\gamma_{1}}+2c_{4}h^{\gamma_{4}}\big)\big((\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}c_{2}h^{\gamma_{2}}+1\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(44)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
2.7
.
∎

3
Moment estimates for tamed exponential Euler approximations

In this section we derive moment estimates for tamed exponential Euler approximations.

Lemma

3.3

and
Lemma

3.4
show well-known moment estimates
for stochastic integrals.
Proposition
3.8

is the main result of this section
and establishes
exponential moments bounds
for tamed exponential Euler approximations.
This is an essential step to prove convergence rates for
tamed exponential Euler approximations.
In the next lemmas we verify the assumptions of Proposition
3.8
.
First we show in Lemma
3.9
that all moments
are bounded.
Lemma
3.12
then establishes
a lifting result, which
lifts moment bounds to moment bounds with more regularity.
Moreover, Lemma
3.11
shows that
tamed exponential Euler approximations are Hölder continuous which we
need in
Lemma
3.12
.
Finally, we derive in Lemma
3.13

one step exponential moment
estimates
of integrated tamed exponential Euler approximations
with respect to a more regular norm.

Throughout this section the following setting is frequently used.

Setting 3.1
.

Assume Setting
2.1
,
let
\eta\in(0,\infty)
,
let

F\colon[0,T]\times H\to H
and

B\colon[0,T]\times H\to HS(U,H)

be measurable satisfying that

\sup_{(t,x)\in[0,T]\times H}\|B(t,x)\|_{HS(U,H)}\leq\eta,

(45)

let
D\in\mathcal{B}(H)
,
let
\Pi\in\operatorname{C}^{2}(H,H)
,
let
\kappa\colon[0,T]\to[0,T]
, be increasing
and satisfy

\kappa(0)=0
,
that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(t-\kappa(t))\leq|\theta|
,
and for all
t\in[0,T]
that
\kappa(t)\leq t
,
let
Y\colon[0,T]\times\Omega\to H
,
be an adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths
satisfying that for all
t\in[0,T]
it holds a.s. that

\begin{split}Y_{t}={}&e^{(t-s)A}Y_{s}+\int^{t}_{s}e^{(t-u)A}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\kappa(u)})F(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&+\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\kappa(u)})\big((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})(AX_{\kappa(u),u})-A\Pi(X_{\kappa(u),u})\big)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&+\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\kappa(u)})(D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})(B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)}))\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\\
&+\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\kappa(u)})\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}}(D^{2}\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})\Big(B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})\tilde{e}_{i},B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})\tilde{e}_{i}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}u.\end{split}

(46)

and for every
s\in[0,T]
let

X_{s}\colon[s,T]\times\Omega\to H
be an

(\mathbb{F}_{t})_{t\in[s,T]}
-adapted stochastic process with continuous sample paths
satisfying that for all
t\in[s,T]
it holds a.s. that

X_{s,t}=\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}.

(47)

The next lemma derives another representations of the mild Itô processes
in

Eq.

46
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume Setting
3.1

and let

\kappa=([0,T]\ni t\to\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}\in[0,T])
.
Then
it holds for all
t\in[0,T]

a.s. that

\displaystyle\begin{split}Y_{t}={}&e^{(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})A}Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}+\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}F(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\Pi\Big(\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}B(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big).\end{split}

(48)

Proof.

Ito’s formula ensures that for all
s\in[0,T]
and all
t\in[s,T]
it holds a.s. that

\begin{split}\Pi(X_{s,t})=&\int_{s}^{t}(D\Pi)(X_{s,u})(AX_{s,u})\,\mathrm{d}u+\int_{s}^{t}(D\Pi)(X_{s,u})(B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}))\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\\
&+\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}}(D^{2}\Pi)(X_{s,u})\Big(B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tilde{e}_{i},B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tilde{e}_{i}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
=&\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}\big((D\Pi)(X_{s,u})(AX_{s,u})-A\Pi(X_{s,u})\big)\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&+\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}(D\Pi)(X_{s,u})(B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}))\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\\
&+\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}}(D^{2}\Pi)(X_{s,u})\Big(B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tilde{e}_{i},B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\tilde{e}_{i}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}u.\end{split}

(49)

Thus, (
46
) implies for all
t\in[0,T]
it holds a.s. that

\begin{split}Y_{t}={}&e^{(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})A}Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}+\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-u)A}F(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}u\\
&+\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\Pi\Big(\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}B(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big),\end{split}

(50)

which completes the proof of Lemma
3.2
.
∎

The next three lemmas derive
moment estimates of stochastic integrals.

Lemma 3.3
.

Assume Setting
3.1
,
let
p\in[2,\infty)
,
\delta\in[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})
,

\gamma\in[\delta,\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})
.
Then it holds for all
u\in[0,T]
and all
t\in[u,T]
that

\|X_{u,t}\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\delta})}\\
\leq{}\eta^{2}\,p^{2}(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2(\delta-\gamma)}(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{2\gamma}\tfrac{(t-u)^{1-2\gamma}}{1-2\gamma}.

(51)

Proof.

Note that
the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality
(see e.g. Lemma 7.7 in
[
17
]
),
the fact that

\forall\lambda\in(0,\infty)\colon\lambda^{\gamma}e^{-\lambda}\leq(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{\gamma}
,
and (
45
)
verify
that for all

u\in[0,T]
and all
t\in[u,T]
it holds that

\begin{split}&\|X_{u,t}\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\delta})}\\
=&\Big\|\int^{t}_{u}e^{(t-s)A}B(\kappa(s),Y_{\kappa(s)})\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\delta})}\\
\leq{}&\tfrac{p(p-1)}{2}\int^{t}_{u}\big\|e^{(t-s)A}B(\kappa(s),Y_{\kappa(s)})\big\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H_{\delta}))}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&\tfrac{p(p-1)}{2}\int^{t}_{u}\big\|(-A)^{\gamma}e^{(t-s)A}\big\|^{2}_{L(H_{\delta},H_{\delta})}\|B(\kappa(s),Y_{\kappa(s)})\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H_{\delta-\gamma}))}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&\tfrac{p(p-1)}{2}\int^{t}_{u}\big(\sup_{\lambda\in(0,\infty)}\lambda^{\gamma}e^{-(t-s)\lambda}\big)^{2}\|B(\kappa(s),Y_{\kappa(s)})\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H_{\delta-\gamma}))}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&p^{2}\int^{t}_{u}(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{2\gamma}(t-s)^{-2\gamma}(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2(\delta-\gamma)}\|B(\kappa(s),Y_{\kappa(s)})\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&p^{2}(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2(\delta-\gamma)}(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{2\gamma}\int^{t}_{u}(t-s)^{-2\gamma}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\eta^{2}\\
={}&\eta^{2}\,p^{2}\,(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2(\delta-\gamma)}(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{2\gamma}\tfrac{(t-u)^{1-2\gamma}}{1-2\gamma}.\end{split}

(52)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
3.3
.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Assume Setting
3.1
,
let

\kappa=([0,T]\ni t\to\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}\in[0,T])
, and
let
p\in[2,\infty)
.
Then it holds for all
u\in[0,T]
that

\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1/2})}\\
\leq{}\sqrt{2p}\,\eta.

(53)

Proof.

The
Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see, e.g., Theorem A in
[
9
]
)
and (
45
)
verify for all
u\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1/2})}={}\Big\|\int_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{u}e^{(u-s)A}B(\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1/2})}\\
\leq{}&2\sqrt{p}\Big\|\Big(\int_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{u}\|e^{(u-s)A}(-A)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}B(\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
={}&2\sqrt{p}\Big\|\Big(\int_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{u}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}-e^{2(u-s)\lambda_{i}}\lambda_{i}\|B^{*}(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})e_{i}\|_{U}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
={}&2\sqrt{p}\Big\|\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(1-e^{2(u-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta})\lambda_{i}})\|B^{*}(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})e_{i}\|_{U}^{2}\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\sqrt{2p}\Big\|\Big(\|B^{*}(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|^{2}_{HS(H,U)}\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\leq{}\sqrt{2p}\,\eta.\end{split}

(54)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
3.4
.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

Assume Setting
3.1
,
let
c,c_{1},\varsigma\in(0,\infty]
,

\gamma_{2}\in[0,\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}]
,

p\in[2,\infty)
,

\alpha\in[\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},1)
,
assume that either
\kappa=\operatorname{Id}
or

\kappa=([0,T]\ni t\to\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}\in[0,T])
,
let
L\colon H\to L(H,H)
be measurable
such that for all
x\in H
and all
i\in\mathcal{I}
it holds that

\|L(x)\|_{L(H,H)}\leq c_{1}

(55)

and that

L(x)e_{i}=\langle L(x)e_{i},e_{i}\rangle_{H}e_{i},

(56)

and assume that for all
t,s\in[0,T]
, and all
x,y\in H
it holds that

\displaystyle\|Y_{t}-Y_{s}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\leq c\,|t-s|^{1-\alpha},

(57)

\displaystyle\|B(t,x)-B(s,y)\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\leq\varsigma^{2}(\|x-y\|^{2}_{H}+|t-s|^{2-2\alpha}),

(58)

\displaystyle\|(-A)^{\gamma_{2}}((D\Pi)(x)-L(x))\|_{L(H,H)}\leq c\|x\|_{H_{\gamma_{2}}}.

(59)

Then it holds for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}\big((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})(B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)}))\big)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1/2})}\\
\leq{}&2\sqrt{p}\Big(c_{1}\eta+c_{1}\varsigma(c+1)\tfrac{t^{1-\alpha}}{\sqrt{2-2\alpha}}+(\tfrac{1-2\gamma_{2}}{2e})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\gamma_{2}}c\eta^{2}\,p(\tfrac{\gamma_{2}}{e})^{\gamma_{2}}t^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\gamma_{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma_{2}}}\Big).\end{split}

(60)

Proof.

First note, that get from the
Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (see, e.g., Theorem A in
[
9
]
)
for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}\big((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})(B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)}))\big)\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1/2})}\\
\leq{}&\Big\|2\sqrt{p}\,\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\big\|e^{(t-u)A}(-A)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\big((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})(B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)}))\big)\big\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
={}&\Big\|2\sqrt{p}\,\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u}))^{*}e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}.\end{split}

(61)

Moreover, the triangle inequality
implies for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u}))^{*}e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
\leq{}&\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})\,L^{*}(X_{\kappa(u),u})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
&+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|(B^{*}(t,Y_{t})-B^{*}(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)}))L^{*}(X_{\kappa(u),u})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
&+\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})-L(X_{\kappa(u),u}))^{*}e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}.\end{split}

(62)

We now estimate each term separately.
For the first term we get from (
56
),
from (
55
),
and from (
45
)
that fot all
t\in[0,T]
it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})\,L^{*}(X_{\kappa(u),u})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
={}&\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\langle L(X_{\kappa(u),u})e_{i},e_{i}\rangle^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
\leq{}&\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,c_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}u={}\tfrac{c_{1}^{2}}{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(1-e^{2t\lambda_{i}})(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\\
\leq{}&c_{1}^{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})e_{i}\big\|_{U}^{2}={}c_{1}^{2}\big\|B(t,Y_{t})\big\|_{HS(U,H)}^{2}\leq c_{1}^{2}\eta^{2}.\end{split}

(63)

Next note, that
the fact that

\forall\lambda\in(0,\infty)\colon\lambda^{\gamma-\gamma_{2}}e^{-\lambda}\leq(\tfrac{\gamma-\gamma_{2}}{e})^{\gamma-\gamma_{2}}
,
(
45
),
and (
59
)
yield for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})-L(X_{\kappa(u),u}))^{*}e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
\leq{}&\int_{0}^{t}\sup_{\lambda\in(0,\infty)}(e^{-(t-u)\lambda}\lambda^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\gamma_{2}})^{2}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}\big\|B^{*}(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})-L(X_{\kappa(u),u}))^{*}(-A)^{\gamma_{2}}e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
\leq{}&\int_{0}^{t}(\tfrac{1-2\gamma_{2}}{2e})^{1-2\gamma_{2}}(t-u)^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\big\|B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})\big\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\big\|(-A)^{\gamma_{2}}((D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})-L(X_{\kappa(u),u}))\big\|^{2}_{L(H,H)}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
\leq{}&(\tfrac{1-2\gamma_{2}}{2e})^{1-2\gamma_{2}}\eta^{2}c^{2}\int_{0}^{t}(t-u)^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\big\|X_{\kappa(u),u}\big\|^{2}_{H_{\gamma_{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}u.\end{split}

(64)

In addition, Lemma
3.3

(with
\gamma\curvearrowleft\gamma_{2}

and with
\delta\curvearrowleft\gamma_{2}
)
implies for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(t-u)^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\big\|X_{\kappa(u),u}\big\|^{2}_{H_{\gamma_{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}={}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}(t-u)^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\big\|X_{\kappa(u),u}\big\|^{2}_{H_{\gamma_{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big\|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}_{L^{p/2}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\
\leq{}&\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(t-u)^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\big\|X_{\kappa(u),u}\big\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\gamma_{2}})}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\leq{}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(t-u)^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\eta^{2}\,p^{2}(\tfrac{\gamma_{2}}{e})^{2\gamma_{2}}\tfrac{(u-\kappa(u))^{1-2\gamma_{2}}}{1-2\gamma_{2}}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
\leq{}&\eta\,p(\tfrac{\gamma_{2}}{e})^{\gamma_{2}}t^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\gamma_{2}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\gamma_{2}}}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}(t-u)^{2\gamma_{2}-1}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}={}\eta\,p(\tfrac{\gamma_{2}}{e})^{\gamma_{2}}t^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\gamma_{2}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\gamma_{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma_{2}}}t^{\gamma_{2}}.\end{split}

(65)

In addition, it holds for all

t\in(0,T]
and all

that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{\lambda\in(0,\infty)}\big(e^{-2(t-u)\lambda}-e^{-2(t-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta})\lambda}\big)=\sup_{\lambda\in(0,\infty)}\big(e^{-2(t-u)\lambda}(1-e^{-2(u-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta})\lambda})\big)\\
={}&e^{-2(t-u)(\ln(t-u)-\ln(t-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}))/(2u-2\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta})}(1-\tfrac{t-u}{t-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\leq(1-\tfrac{t-u}{t-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})=\tfrac{u-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}{t-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}.\end{split}

(66)

Therefore,
(

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in\theta\cup\{t\}}\mathbbm{1}_{[0,t]}(u)\int_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{u}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-r)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})e_{i}-B^{*}(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}\langle L(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},r})e_{i},e_{i}\rangle^{2}_{H}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in\theta\cup\{t\}}\mathbbm{1}_{[0,t]}(u)\int_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{u}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}e^{2(t-r)\lambda_{i}}(-\lambda_{i})\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})e_{i}-B^{*}(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}c_{1}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\tfrac{c_{1}^{2}}{2}\sum_{u\in\theta\cup\{t\}}\mathbbm{1}_{[0,t]}(u)\sum_{i\in\mathcal{I}}(e^{2(t-u)\lambda_{i}}-e^{2(t-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta})\lambda_{i}})\big\|B^{*}(t,Y_{t})e_{i}-B^{*}(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})e_{i}\big\|^{2}_{U}

(67)

) assures

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\Big(c_{1}^{2}\varsigma^{2}\sum_{u\in\theta\cup\{t\}}\mathbbm{1}_{[0,t]}(u)\tfrac{u-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}{t-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}\big(\big\|Y_{t}-Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}\big\|^{2}_{H}+|t-\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}|^{2-2\alpha}\big)\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})}\\

\leq{}&c_{1}\varsigma(c+1)\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\tfrac{1}{(t-s)^{2\alpha-1}}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}={}c_{1}\varsigma(c+1)\tfrac{t^{1-\alpha}}{\sqrt{2-2\alpha}}.\end{split}

\leq{}&2\sqrt{p}\Big(c_{1}\eta+c_{1}\varsigma(c+1)\tfrac{t^{1-\alpha}}{\sqrt{2-2\alpha}}+(\tfrac{1-2\gamma_{2}}{2e})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\gamma_{2}}c\eta^{2}\,p(\tfrac{\gamma_{2}}{e})^{\gamma_{2}}t^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-2\gamma_{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2\gamma_{2}}}\Big).\end{split}

The following lemma allows us to quantify how close
\Pi
and the identity function are.

Lemma 3.6
.

Assume Setting
3.1
,
let

\kappa=([0,T]\ni t\to\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}\in[0,T])
,
let
p\in[2,\infty)
,

c_{1}\in[0,\infty)
,

c_{2}\in[1,\infty)
,
and assume for all
x\in H
that

\displaystyle\|(D\Pi)(x)(Ax)-A\Pi(x)\|_{H}\leq c_{1}\|x\|_{H_{1/2}}(\|x\|_{H_{1/2}}+1)(\|x\|_{H}+1)^{c_{2}}.

(72)

Then it holds for all
u\in[0,T]
that

\Big\|(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(AX_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})-A\Pi(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\leq c_{1}\,\sqrt{6p}\,\eta(\sqrt{6p}\,\eta+1)(1+3p\,\eta\,c_{2}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})^{c_{2}}.

(73)

Proof.

First note that (
72
) implies
for all
u\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(AX_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})-A\Pi(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\\
\leq{}&\big(\mathbbm{E}\big[c_{1}^{p}\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|^{p}_{H_{1/2}}(\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|_{H_{1/2}}+1)^{p}(\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|_{H}+1)^{c_{2}p}\big]\big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}}\\
\leq{}&c_{1}\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|_{L^{3p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1/2})}(\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|_{L^{3p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1/2})}+1)(\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|^{c_{2}}_{L^{3pc_{2}}(\mathbbm{P};H)}+1).\end{split}

(74)

Moreover,
Lemma
3.4
(with
p\curvearrowleft 3p
)
verify for all
u\in[0,T]
that

\|X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u}\|_{L^{3p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1/2})}\leq{}\sqrt{6p}\,\eta.

(75)

Thus, (
74
),
Lemma
3.3
(with
p\curvearrowleft 3pc_{2}
,
\delta\curvearrowleft 0
and

\gamma\curvearrowleft 0
),
and the fact that for all
a,b,c\in[0,\infty)
it holds that

(a+b+c)^{1/p}\leq a^{1/p}+b^{1/p}+c^{1/p}

show for all
u\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(AX_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})-A\Pi(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\\
\leq{}&c_{1}\,\sqrt{6p}\,\eta(\sqrt{6p}\,\eta+1)(1+3p\,\eta\,c_{2}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})^{c_{2}}.\end{split}

(76)

This finishes the proof of Lemma
3.6
.
∎

In the next lemma
we approximate the stepsize of tamed exponential Euler approximations

Lemma 3.7
.

Assume Setting
3.1
,
let
c_{1},c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
,

,

,

,

,

\kappa=([0,T]\ni t\to\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}\in[0,T])
,

and all

that

\displaystyle\|x\|_{H_{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}},

(77)

\displaystyle\|F(t,x)\|_{H_{-\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}},

(78)

and assume for all

, and all

that

\displaystyle\|\Pi(x)\|_{H}\leq c_{1}\|x\|_{H}.

(79)

Then it holds for all

that

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})(Y_{t}-Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P},H_{\alpha})}\leq{}c_{2}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha+\gamma_{1}}+c_{2}(\tfrac{\alpha+\beta}{e})^{\alpha+\beta}\tfrac{|\theta|^{1-\alpha-\beta+\gamma_{1}}}{1-\alpha-\beta}+c_{1}\eta\,p(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}\tfrac{|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha}}{\sqrt{1-2\alpha}}.

(80)

Proof.

Note that (
79
),

2.2

(with

,

\delta\curvearrowleft\alpha
,

\gamma\curvearrowleft\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha
),

,
(with

,

,

),

3.3

(with

,

),
(
77
)
and (

)
verify for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})(Y_{t}-Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P},H_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\Big(e^{(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})A}Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}-Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}+\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}F(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\qquad+\Pi\Big(\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}B(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big)\Big)\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P},H_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})(e^{(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})A}-\operatorname{Id}_{H})Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P},H_{\alpha})}+\Big\|\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}F(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}s\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P},H_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\qquad+c_{1}\Big\|\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}e^{(t-s)A}B(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P},H_{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\|\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P},H_{1/2})}(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha}+(\tfrac{\alpha+\beta}{e})^{\alpha+\beta}\tfrac{(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})^{1-\alpha-\beta}}{1-\alpha-\beta}\|\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})F(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|_{H_{-\beta}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\,p(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}\tfrac{(t-u)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha}}{\sqrt{1-2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle c_{2}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha+\gamma_{1}}+c_{2}(\tfrac{\alpha+\beta}{e})^{\alpha+\beta}\tfrac{|\theta|^{1-\alpha-\beta+\gamma_{1}}}{1-\alpha-\beta}+c_{1}\eta\,p(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}\tfrac{|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha}}{\sqrt{1-2\alpha}}.

(81)

In the next proposition,

For this,

Proposition 3.8
.

Assume Setting
3.1
,
let

,

,

,

,

,

\alpha\in(\beta-1,0]
,

\gamma\in(\beta,1]
,

\gamma_{1}\in[-\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}},0)
,

\gamma_{2}\in[-\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}},0]
,

\gamma_{3}\in[0,\infty)
,

\gamma_{4}\in[\tfrac{-1-2\gamma_{1}}{2c_{7}},\gamma_{1}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}]
,

\gamma_{5}\in[0,\infty)
,
satisfy that

-2\gamma_{2}\leq(\gamma-\beta)\wedge(1-\beta+\alpha)\wedge\gamma_{5},

(82)

c_{4}=\big(2c_{2}+c_{5}\big)\vee\big(8c_{5}\eta^{2}+\varsigma(3c_{2}+8c_{5}\eta+1)+\eta\big),

(83)

and that

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{1}\geq{}&c_{2}\big(c_{2}+\tfrac{2c_{2}}{1-2\beta}(\tfrac{1/2+\beta}{e})^{\beta}+\tfrac{4c_{5}\eta}{\sqrt{1-2\beta}}(\tfrac{\beta}{e})^{\beta}+1\big)(1+2c_{2}^{c_{9}})\\
&\quad+(\inf_{i\in\mathcal{I}}|\lambda_{i}|)^{\alpha}\big(2c_{5}\,\eta^{3}+2\sqrt{6}\,c_{5}\eta(2\sqrt{6}\,\eta+1)(1+12\,\eta\,c_{8})^{c_{8}}\big)\\
&\quad+8c_{5}\eta^{2}+\varsigma(3c_{2}+8c_{5}\eta+1)\end{split}

(84)

assume

|\theta|\leq 1
,
let

\kappa=([0,T]\ni t\to\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}\in[0,T])
,
let
V\in\operatorname{C}^{1,2}([0,T]\times H,[0,\infty))
,
let

\overline{V}\colon[0,T]\times H\to\mathbbm{R}
be measurable,
assume for all
t\in[0,T]
,
s\in[t,T]
, and all
x,y\in H
that

\displaystyle\|F(s,x)-F(t,y)\|_{H_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}(1+\|x\|^{c_{9}}_{H_{\gamma}}+\|y\|^{c_{9}}_{H_{\gamma}})(\|x-y\|_{H_{\beta}}+|s-t|),

(85)

\displaystyle\|B(s,x)-B(t,y)\|_{HS(U,H)}

\displaystyle\leq\varsigma(\|x-y\|_{H}+(s-t)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}),

(86)

\displaystyle V(s,e^{tA}x)

\displaystyle\leq V(s-t,x),

(87)

assume for all
t\in[0,T]
,
x\in D
that

\displaystyle\|x\|_{H_{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}},

(88)

\displaystyle\|F(t,x)\|_{H_{-1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}},

(89)

\displaystyle V(t,x)

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}},

(90)

assume for all
x\in H
and all
z\in HS(U,H)
that

\displaystyle\|\Pi(x)\|_{H}\leq c_{5}(|\theta|^{\gamma_{4}}\wedge\|x\|_{H}),

(91)

\displaystyle\|\Pi(x)\|_{H_{\beta}}\leq c_{5}\|x\|_{H_{\beta}},

(92)

\displaystyle\|(D\Pi)(x)-\operatorname{Id}_{H}\|_{L(H,H)}\leq c_{5}\|x\|_{H},

(93)

\displaystyle\|(D\Pi)(x)(Ax)-A\Pi(x)\|_{H}\leq c_{5}|\theta|^{\gamma_{5}}\|x\|_{H_{1/2}}(\|x\|_{H_{1/2}}+1)(\|x\|_{H}+1)^{c_{8}},

&\quad+k\cdot(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})\big)\Big)\,2\mathbbm{E}\bigg[\exp\Big(2ek^{2}\int^{t}_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\|B(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}r\Big)~\bigg|~Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}\bigg]\\
\leq{}&\exp\Big(k\,c_{2}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+2k(\tfrac{1}{2e})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}c_{2}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}}+k\cdot|\theta|\Big)2\exp\Big(2ek^{2}\,\eta^{2}|\theta|\Big)\\
\leq{}&2\exp\Big((|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}k+|\theta|^{1+2\gamma_{1}}k^{2})(c_{2}+2c_{2}(\tfrac{1}{2e})^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+1+2e\eta^{2})\Big)\\
\leq{}&2\exp\Big((|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}k+|\theta|^{1+2\gamma_{1}}k^{2})(2c_{2}+1+2e\eta^{2})\Big).\end{split}

(114)

Therefore, (
46
),
Lemma
2.7

(applied with

T\curvearrowleft t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}
,

E\curvearrowleft D
,

\big)_{s\in[0,t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}]}
,

,

c_{6}\curvearrowleft 2c_{2}+1+2e\eta^{2}
,

\gamma_{1}\curvearrowleft-2\gamma_{2}
,

,

,

\gamma_{6}\curvearrowleft\gamma_{1}
,

\gamma_{7}\curvearrowleft\gamma_{4}
,

),
the fact that

,

verify

and all

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\big(\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(t,Y_{t})+\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\int_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{t}\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\big)~\Big|(Y_{r})_{r\in[0,{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}]}\Big]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[e^{\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})}~\Big|(Y_{r})_{r\in[0,{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}]}\Big]\\
&\quad+\int_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}^{t}\Big(\mathbbm{E}\Big[e^{4\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})+(2c_{2}+1+2e\eta^{2})16c^{2}_{5}(1+c_{1}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}|\theta|^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{4}})^{2}|\theta|^{1+2\gamma_{1}}}\\
&\quad\quad\cdot e^{(2c_{2}+1+2e\eta^{2})4c_{5}(1+c_{1}|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}|\theta|^{c_{7}\cdot\gamma_{4}})|\theta|^{\gamma_{1}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}}~\Big|(Y_{r})_{r\in[0,{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}}]}\Big]\Big)^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}}e^{c_{3}(s-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})^{\gamma_{3}}}\\
&\quad\quad\cdot c_{1}c_{2}|\theta|^{\gamma_{2}-2\gamma_{2}}\Big(1+\tfrac{1}{2}\big(c_{1}|\theta|^{-2\gamma_{2}}+2c_{4}\big)\big((\inf_{i\in I}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}c_{2}|\theta|^{\gamma_{2}}+1\big)\Big)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq{}&e^{\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})}\\
&\quad\cdot\Big(1+(t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})\cdot e^{(2c_{2}+1+2e\eta^{2})(4c^{2}_{5}(1+c_{1}+c_{4}^{c_{7}})^{2}+c_{5}(1+c_{1}+c_{4}^{c_{7}}))+c_{3}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\cdot c_{1}c_{2}\big(1+\tfrac{1}{2}(c_{1}+2c_{4})((\inf_{i\in I}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}c_{2}+1)\big)\Big)\\
\leq{}&e^{\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})V(\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})}\exp\Big((t-\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta})\\
&\quad\cdot e^{(2c_{2}+1+2e\eta^{2})5c^{2}_{5}(1+c_{1}+c_{4}^{c_{7}})^{2}+c_{3}}c_{1}c_{2}\big(1+\tfrac{1}{2}(c_{1}+2c_{4})((\inf_{i\in I}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}c_{2}+1)\big)\Big).\end{split}

(115)

Hence, (
87
) and
Lemma
2.6

(with

,

\cdot c_{2}+1))

proves for all

that

\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(V(t,Y_{t})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner s\lrcorner_{\theta}})\overline{V}(s,Y_{s})\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\Big]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\exp\Big(V(0,Y_{0})+t\big(e^{(2c_{2}+1+2e\eta^{2})5c^{2}_{5}(1+c_{1}+c_{4}^{c_{7}})^{2}+c_{3}}\\
&\qquad\cdot c_{1}c_{2}(1+\tfrac{1}{2}(c_{1}+2c_{4})((\inf_{i\in I}|\lambda_{i}|)^{2\alpha}c_{2}+1))\big)\Big)\Big].\end{split}

(116)

This completes the proof of Proposition
3.8
.

In the next two lemma we derive moment estimates for
tamed exponential Euler approximations.

Lemma 3.9
.

Assume Setting
3.1
,
let
c,c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}\in(0,\infty)
,

,

{\varepsilon}\in(0,1]
,

satisfy that

\begin{split}c\geq{}&2pc_{3}\big(c_{2}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\gamma_{1}-\alpha}+c_{2}(1-2\alpha)^{-1}(\tfrac{2\alpha}{e})^{2\alpha}|\theta|^{1-2\alpha+\gamma_{1}}+c_{1}\eta\,p(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}\tfrac{|\theta|^{1/2-\alpha}}{\sqrt{1-2\alpha}}\big)^{2}\\
&+pc_{2}\big(c_{2}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}-\alpha+2\gamma_{1}}+c_{2}(1-2\alpha)^{-1}(\tfrac{2\alpha}{e})^{2\alpha}|\theta|^{1-2\alpha+2\gamma_{1}}+c_{1}\eta\,p(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}\tfrac{|\theta|^{1/2+\gamma_{1}-\alpha}}{\sqrt{1-2\alpha}}\big)\\
&+c_{1}\,p\sqrt{6p}\,\eta(\sqrt{6p}\,\eta+1)(1+3p\,\eta\,c_{4}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}})^{c_{4}}|\theta|^{\gamma_{3}}+c_{1}\,\eta^{3}\,p^{2}\,|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}\\
&+p^{2}\eta^{2}(c_{1}\,\eta\,p|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+1)^{2}+2c_{3}(4\alpha c_{3}{\varepsilon}^{-1})^{2\alpha/(1-2\alpha)}(1-2\alpha),\end{split}

(117)

let

\kappa=([0,T]\ni t\to\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}\in[0,T])

(124)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}}(D^{2}\Pi)(x)\big(z\tilde{e}_{i},z\tilde{e}_{i}\big)\Big\|_{H}\leq c_{1}\|x\|_{H}\cdot\|z\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}.

(125)

Then it holds for all

that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|Y_{t}\|_{H}^{p}+p\,(1-{\varepsilon})\int_{0}^{t}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\|Y_{u}\|^{2}_{H_{1/2}}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big]\leq{}\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|Y_{0}\|_{H}^{p}\Big]e^{ct}.\end{split}

(126)

Proof.

First note that (

) enures that for all

it holds a.s. that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\langle Y_{u},F(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\rangle_{H}\\
={}&\langle Y_{u}-Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}},F(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\rangle_{H}+\langle Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}},F(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\rangle_{H}\\

\leq{}&\|Y_{u}-Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}\|_{H_{\alpha}}\|F(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|_{H_{-\alpha}}+2c_{3}\|Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}-Y_{u}\|_{H_{\alpha}}^{2}+2c_{3}\|Y_{u}\|_{H_{\alpha}}^{2}.\end{split}

(127)

In addition, Lemma
3.7

(with

,

)
verify for all

and all

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})(Y_{t}-Y_{\llcorner t\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{r})}\\
\leq{}&c_{2}|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}+\gamma_{1}-r}+c_{2}(1-r-\alpha)^{-1}(\tfrac{r+\alpha}{e})^{r+\alpha}|\theta|^{1-r-\alpha+\gamma_{1}}+c_{1}\eta\,p(\tfrac{r}{e})^{r}\tfrac{|\theta|^{1/2-r}}{\sqrt{1-2r}}.\end{split}

(128)

Moreover, (
46
) and Ito’s formula imply that for all
t\in[0,T]
it holds a.s. that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|Y_{t}\|_{H}^{p}-\|Y_{0}\|_{H}^{p}\\
={}&\int_{0}^{t}p\|Y_{u}\|_{H}^{p-2}\langle Y_{u},AY_{u}\rangle_{H}+p\,\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\Big(\\
&\quad\Big\langle Y_{u},F(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})+(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(AX_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})-A\Pi(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})\Big\rangle_{H}\\

(129)

Denote by

Then we get from
paths continuity of
Y
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|Y_{t}\|_{H}^{p}+p(1-{\varepsilon})\int_{0}^{t}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\|Y_{u}\|_{H_{1/2}}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big]\\
\leq{}&\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|Y_{t\wedge\tau_{n}}\|_{H}^{p}+p(1-{\varepsilon})\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{n}}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\langle Y_{u},(-A)Y_{u}\rangle_{H}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big]\\
={}&\liminf_{n\to\infty}\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\|Y_{0}\|_{H}^{p}+p\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{n}}{\varepsilon}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\langle Y_{u},AY_{u}\rangle_{H}+\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\Big(\\
&\quad\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\Big\langle Y_{u},F(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})+(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(AX_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})-A\Pi(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})\Big\rangle_{H}\\

&\quad+\tfrac{p-2}{2}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-4}_{H}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}}\big\langle Y_{u},(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}))\tilde{e}_{i}\big\rangle_{H}^{2}\\
&\quad+\tfrac{1}{2}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\big\|(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}))\big\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}u\Bigg]\\
\leq{}&\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\|Y_{0}\|_{H}^{p}+p\int_{0}^{t}2c_{3}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}-Y_{u}\|_{H_{\alpha}}^{2}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}+2c_{3}\|Y_{u}\|_{H_{\alpha}}^{2}\|Y_{u}\|_{H}^{p-2}\\

&\quad+\|Y_{u}\|^{p-1}_{H}\|(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(AX_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})-A\Pi(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})\|_{H}\\

&\quad+\tfrac{p-1}{2}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\big\|(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(B(\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}}))\big\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)}\,\mathrm{d}u\Bigg].\end{split}

(131)

Next note, that the

This

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}2c_{3}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\|Y_{u}\|^{2}_{H_{\alpha}}-{\varepsilon}\|Y_{u}\|^{p-2}_{H}\|Y_{u}\|^{2}_{H_{1/2}}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big]\\

(134)

Moreover,

&\int_{0}^{t}1+\|Y_{u}\|^{p}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\,\mathrm{d}u.\end{split}

(135)

In addition,
Hölder’s inequality and
Lemma
3.6

(with
c_{1}\curvearrowleft c_{1}|\theta|^{\gamma_{3}}
,
c_{2}\curvearrowleft c_{4}
)
show for all
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta}})\|Y_{u}\|^{p-1}_{H}\big\|(D\Pi)(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})(AX_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})-A\Pi(X_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})\big\|_{H}\,\mathrm{d}u\Big]\\

(164)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle 8^{a_{1}/(4-2a_{1})}4^{a_{2}/(2-a_{1})}9^{a_{3}/(2-a_{1})}c_{4}^{2/(2-a_{1})}(c_{5}+1)(c_{5}+\hat{c}+1)\cdot(\|Y_{t}-Z_{t}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P},H_{\gamma})}^{p}+1).

and all

(165)

The next lemma demonstrates that tamed exponential Euler approximations
are Hölder continuous.

Lemma 3.11
.

Assume Setting
3.1
,
let
\alpha\in[\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},1)
,

\alpha_{1}\in\mathbbm{R}
,

c_{1},c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
,
q\in[1,\infty)
,

p\in[2,\infty)
,
let
c\in(0,\infty]
satisfy that

\begin{split}c\geq{}&\sup_{u\in[0,T]}\|Y_{u}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H_{1-\alpha})}+c_{2}(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}(1-\alpha)^{-1}(1+\sup_{u\in[0,T]}\|Y_{u}\|^{q}_{L^{pq}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\alpha_{1}})})+c_{1}\eta^{3}\,p|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}T^{\alpha}\\
&+c_{1}+p\eta\big(2c_{1}\eta\,p|\theta|^{\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}}+1\big)T^{\alpha-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}},\end{split}

(172)

and assume that for all
t\in[0,T]
,

, and all

it holds that

\displaystyle\|F(t,x)\|_{H_{-\alpha}}\leq c_{2}(\|x\|_{H_{\alpha_{1}}}^{q}+1),

(173)

\displaystyle\|\Pi(x)\|_{H}\leq c_{1}\|x\|_{H},

(174)

\displaystyle\|(D\Pi)(x)-\operatorname{Id}_{H}\|_{L(H,H)}\leq c_{1}\|x\|_{H},

(175)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}}(D^{2}\Pi)(x)\big(z\tilde{e}_{i},z\tilde{e}_{i}\big)\Big\|_{H}\leq c_{1}\|x\|_{H}\cdot\|z\|^{2}_{HS(U,H)},

(176)

and assume that

\Big\|(D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})(AX_{\llcorner u\lrcorner_{\theta},u})-A\Pi(X_{\kappa(u),u})\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\leq c_{1}.

(177)

Then it holds for all
s,t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|Y_{t}-Y_{s}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\leq{}c\,|t-s|^{1-\alpha}.\end{split}

(178)

Proof.

First note, that Lemma
2.2

(with

,

,

, and with

) ensures

and all

(179)

In addition,
(
173
),
the fact that

\forall\gamma\in[0,1],\forall\lambda\in(0,\infty)\colon\lambda^{\gamma}e^{-\lambda}\leq(\tfrac{\gamma}{e})^{\gamma}
,

\forall a,b\in[0,\infty)\colon(a+b)^{1/p}\leq a^{1/p}+b^{1/p}

imply for all

and all

that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\int^{t}_{s}e^{(t-u)A}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\kappa(u)})F(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})\,\mathrm{d}u\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\\
\leq{}&\int^{t}_{s}\|e^{(t-u)A}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\kappa(u)})F(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
\leq{}&\int^{t}_{s}\|e^{(t-u)A}(-A)^{\alpha}\|_{L(H,H)}\|(-A)^{-\alpha}F(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
\leq{}&c_{2}\int^{t}_{s}\big(\sup_{\lambda\in(0,\infty)}\lambda^{\alpha}e^{-(t-u)\lambda}\big)(1+\big\|\|Y_{\kappa(u)}\|_{H_{\alpha_{1}}}^{q}\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};\mathbbm{R})})\,\mathrm{d}u\\
\leq{}&c_{2}\int^{t}_{s}(t-u)^{-\alpha}(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}(1+\|Y_{\kappa(u)}\|^{q}_{L^{pq}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\alpha_{1}})})\,\mathrm{d}u\\
\leq{}&c_{2}(1+\sup_{u\in[0,T]}\|Y_{u}\|^{q}_{L^{pq}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\alpha_{1}})})\int^{t}_{s}(t-u)^{-\alpha}(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}u\\
={}&c_{2}(\tfrac{\alpha}{e})^{\alpha}(1-\alpha)^{-1}(1+\sup_{u\in[0,T]}\|Y_{u}\|^{q}_{L^{pq}(\mathbbm{P};H_{\alpha_{1}})})(t-s)^{1-\alpha}.\end{split}

(180)

Furthermore,
the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality
(see e.g. Lemma 7.7 in
[
17
]
)
(
175
), (
45
)
Lemma

(with

and

)
and

\alpha\geq\tfrac{1}{2}
verify
for all

and all

that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|\int_{s}^{t}e^{(t-u)A}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\kappa(u)})(D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})(B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)}))\,\mathrm{d}W_{u}\Big\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};H)}\\
\leq{}&\tfrac{p(p-1)}{2}\int_{s}^{t}\big\|e^{(t-u)A}\mathbbm{1}_{D}(Y_{\kappa(u)})(D\Pi)(X_{\kappa(u),u})(B(\kappa(u),Y_{\kappa(u)}))\big\|^{2}_{L^{p}(\mathbbm{P};HS(U,H))}\,\mathrm{d}u\\

(181)

In addition,
(

(

),

and

)
show
